# Spark — Task Register

Every task, its epic, and its state. Epics are described in [EPICS.md](EPICS.md); priority
order for what to do next is in [TODO.md](TODO.md); the requirements behind them are in
[PRD.md](PRD.md).

**Last updated:** 2026-09-13 (`E2-T72`: the tangent-constrained interpolation)
**Legend:** `Done` · `In progress` · `Open` · `Blocked` · `Withdrawn`

**Summary:** 360 done · 8 in progress · 21 open · 6 blocked · 2 deferred · 11 withdrawn — **408 rows**

## How to read this file

**`In progress` means part-delivered with the missing part named on the row** — never "started",
and never "finished but unfiled". A row that cannot name what is left of it does not belong in
that column, and on 2026-09-09 the whole column was audited against the source tree to make that
true again: every remaining `In progress` row below says, in a sentence a stranger could act on,
what is not built yet.

**Every `Done` row was verified by reading the repository and running the gates**, not by
recollection. On 2026-09-09 all three are green on this tree: `dotnet build Spark.slnx
--no-incremental -warnaserror` clean over sixteen projects with zero warnings, **2,964 tests
across ten test executables with zero failures and zero skips**, and `dotnet format Spark.slnx
--verify-no-changes --severity warn` clean. **Two counts in that sentence were wrong for eleven
days and are worth the correction**: the audit first said *2,966 across ten*, because the
verification loop was executing the leftover binary of a deleted project ([N135](NOTES.md)).
The zero skip count matters on its own: `Spark.Geometry.Occt.Tests` skips itself when the native
shim is absent, so a green managed run without it proves nothing about the provider.

**Nothing runs in CI any more.** GitHub Actions was switched off for this repository on
2026-09-09 (`E13-T19`) once it went private and Actions minutes stopped being free
(`E12-T22`, `E13-T20`). Four rows below were held open *solely* by a CI job that had never
executed — `E1-T21`, `E8-T15`, `E11-T14` and `E11-T16` — and holding a row open against a
workflow that can no longer run is a promise nobody can keep. Each now says what it actually
delivers today, what it stopped being able to prove, and what would close it if Actions is ever
turned back on.

### The lesson this register exists to carry

The first attempt at the geometry value layer **passed all three gates and was rejected** by an
independent review, which found three of its eight claims false — most damningly that
`default(Plane).Contains(anyPoint)` returned `true`, so every point in space silently lay on the
null plane. Both tests written to guard the type were **structurally incapable of failing**.
**Gates are not review**, and the standard that came out of it holds every fix since: *a fix is
regression-proven by reverting it and naming the test that goes red.*

### Withdrawn rows, and why they are still here

**Ten rows are `Withdrawn`, in three groups**, kept rather than deleted because a task that
vanishes silently is a task somebody re-adds.

- **Four are about publishing Spark's own assemblies to nuget.org** — `E1-T24`, `E12-T6`,
  `E12-T7`, `E12-T17`. **D11** settles that Spark *consumes* NuGet packages and publishes none.
- **Five come from ADR-0020**, the decision to take OpenCascade rather than write a solid kernel:
  `E2-T24` (sew, heal, validate), `E2-T25` (extrude, revolve, loft, sweep), `E2-T36` (the managed
  STEP subset) and `E2-T37` (the throwaway SSI spike) are work **discarded rather than descoped**;
  `E12-T15` because the sentence it existed to say publicly — *exact booleans are out of scope for
  1.0* — stopped being true.
- **One is `E8-T81`**, withdrawn into `E11-T27` when the two turned out to be one defect.

**`E2-T27` is reduced, not withdrawn, and the difference matters.** Mesh booleans move to 1.x with
`Capabilities` greying them, because **OCCT is poor at mesh booleans and Dynamo has them**.
Reading that row as *cancelled* would be a mistake this paragraph exists to prevent.

### The Dynamo parity rows

`E2-T40` … `E2-T46` are one row per subsystem and carry no detail of their own;
[DYNAMO-COVERAGE.md](DYNAMO-COVERAGE.md) is their detail — a metadata-only read of the
`ProtoGeometry.dll` installed with Revit 2026, **51 public types and 837 public members**.
`E11-T23` is the two-way diff test that stops that register becoming the next `DocGenerator`:
837 hand-maintained Markdown rows are the same artefact that drifted until 101 of 108
constructors rendered blank. **Five subsystems have now been re-counted member by member against
the assembly** — §3.3 surfaces (`E2-T42`, 48 of 106), §3.4 solids (`E2-T43`, 24 of 55), §3.5
topology (`E2-T44`, 31 of 33), §3.6 mesh (`E2-T45`, 41 of 65) and §3.8 infrastructure (`E2-T46`,
24 of 89) — and every one of them was reading *0 reachable* or near it for capabilities that
already worked. **What is left is curves (§3.2) and the value layer (§3.1)**, which is `E2-T41`'s
and `E2-T40`'s job, plus `Q12`'s T-Splines decision. The lesson each of the four taught is the same and is worth stating once: the
register lies in the *safe* direction, and only reading the assembly catches it.

Everything else in this file is a plan, not a claim.

From here a shipped member that changes or vanishes is a diff in a file somebody has to explain, which is what makes AGENTS' SemVer rule readable rather than remembered. Kept as a **review aid, not a compatibility guarantee** — nothing is published, so nobody's restore breaks; what the baseline prevents is a public surface growing by accident (ADR-0019). Half the mechanism behind [E11-T14](#e11--quality-and-verification) |

The remaining three are [E1-T30](#e1--foundations-build-and-ci) |

Withdraws PRD Q9 |
